A fresh worktree has no `corpus/files`: pass `--corpus /home/agent/workspace/safe-pdf/corpus/files`.
* 2026-11-28 (13.10) — Never run `lake -Kasserts=1` in the release workspace: Lake caches the elaborated lakefile with the `-K` options and a later plain `lake build` reuses them (only `-R` reconfigures); `scripts/asserts.sh` builds in a separate workspace (`.lake/asserts-ws`, git-ignored). `jpeg2000`'s flags are set through `CCC_OVERRIDE_OPTIONS='s/^-DNDEBUG$/-UNDEBUG/'` (cosmetic `###` lines in the log). Build proof: objects referencing `lean_notify_assert` — safe-pdf 169/172, jpeg2000 47/47, release 0/119. `assert-rig array|bytes` abort with exit 134 under it (the same read segfaults or returns garbage in release). CI job `asserts` (60 min cap, `FUZZ_LONG_SECONDS=90`) has not run on GitHub; cold-build time on a runner unknown. Only generated C and `lean.h` inline functions are covered; the prebuilt runtime stays a release build (say so in `docs/safety.md`, task 13.21). `lakefile.lean` gained `assertsBuild`, a `-UNDEBUG` in `moreLeancArgs` and a non-default `lean_exe «assert-rig»`.
* 2026-11-28 (13.27) — `Select.normChars` copied its array on every push because the fold's pair accumulator stayed live (`acc.1` read while `acc.2` was used in the closure); destructuring `fun (out, i) c => …` fixes it (proof-patterns §4.12); 2,048,000 characters now take 54 ms (ratio 1.96). IR not traced; timing is the evidence.
* 2026-11-28 (13.28) — `SafePdf/Shell/App/Xfdf.lean` had no theorems, only `#guard`s: the rewrites (`importItems` with ordered `seen`/`taken` sets of an injective `List Int` mark key, `toXfdf` with a one-pass `CommentIndex`) are NOT proved equal to the originals (kept as `importItemsSpec` / `toXfdfSpec`); the equality is checked by `#guard`s on stores covering comments, older notes, free text, ink, repeated and reversed items, ids below `nextId`. The proof would need lawful ordering on `List Int` plus injectivity of the key flattening. A `def` returning a closure over `let`-bound indexes can rebuild them on every call in compiled code: return an explicit structure.
* 2026-11-28 (13.24) — Resource lookups: `SafePdf/Core/DictIndex.lean` (`Dict.indexWith` via `Std.TreeMap.insertIfNew`, first entry wins; `Dict.indexWith_get?`/`Dict.index_get?` equal `Dict.lookup` for every dictionary including duplicates; `Dict.getIdx_index` for `Dict.get?`), `ResIdx` (lazy Thunk index with a proof field), `Frame.idx` built through `Frame.mk'` (every `⟨res, …⟩` constructor had to change: `Annot.lean`, `Raster/Tiling.lean`, `Raster/Type3.lean`, `Tests/JPXTint.lean`), `resEntryI_eq`, `loadFonts_eq` (`loadStep`/`loadFontsSpec` kept), `Doc.dests : DestsIdx` built once at open with `destsLookup_eq` (falls back to the scan when the limits differ). For proofs about thunks use the `thunk_get_mk` lemma: `show`/`rfl` on a thunk body makes `whnf` time out. `dests-names` is capped by `maxAnnots` = 4096.

## Decisions and deviations from the design document

* 2026-11-26 — For JPXDecode rendering, allow one Lake dependency, the pure Lean
  `jpeg2000` package at `9a8880a8604dbebf93b99253e7965d54673dc534`.
  This M7-based revision adds a Lake-only packaging switch. Set `libraryOnly=true`
  so safe-pdf's `Tests.*` modules resolve locally. The package uses the same
  Lean 4.36.0 toolchain, has zero Lake dependencies, and provides
  typed decode errors and explicit size budgets. The PDF dictionary, compositing
  rules and worker boundary stay with safe-pdf. Review pin changes together with
  the PDF rendering fixtures.
* 2026-11-23 (9.r11a) — **Annotations with `/OC` follow ISO 32000-2 §8.11 and PDF.js, not MuPDF**: `AllOn` over groups including an OFF one hides, `/VE` is evaluated, the default configuration is `/D` even when `/Configs` exists. MuPDF differs in these cases, so pixel comparisons against MuPDF may disagree on such files.
* 2026-11-23 (Phase 9, human) — **The viewer honours every document open setting** (`/PageLayout`, `/PageMode`, `/OpenAction` as a destination/GoTo only, `/ViewerPreferences`) on every open; without them it opens in Vertical Scrolling at page 1. A mode the user picks lasts until the next open. **User defaults via a preferences page are planned later by the human** (not in scope yet). Never executed from `/OpenAction`: JavaScript, Launch, URI or any non-GoTo action (S-rules: no scripting, no network, no launching).
* 2026-11-23 (Phase 9, human) — **Save overwrites the opened file after a confirmation** (supersedes "to a new file, never in place" for the viewer's Save; Plan.md S8 amended with the human's agreement). The core is unchanged: it still returns `bytes ++ update` (C7); the shell writes that to a temporary file in the same directory, re-parses it with the strict parser, then renames it over the original, so the original bytes remain a prefix of the file and a crash leaves either the old or the new file. Save As… still writes a new file.
* 2026-11-18 (0.1) — Toolchain pinned to `leanprover/lean4:v4.36.0`; **no Lake dependencies** (no Mathlib, no Batteries) to start, for build speed and a small trusted base. Termination measures that Plan.md §5.2 sketches with `Finset ℕ` use core-Lean structures (e.g. `List Nat`/bitmaps with explicit size measures) instead. Adding Mathlib later needs a new Decisions entry.
* 2026-11-18 (0.1) — `autoImplicit = false` project-wide.
* 2026-11-18 (1.1) — Object model decisions: direct/indirect split (streams never nested); exact decimal reals, never Float (round-trip); decoded `List UInt8` strings/names for proof ergonomics; strict parser **rejects duplicate dictionary keys** (S6; ISO 32000-2 §7.3.7 leaves them undefined), repair path may keep the last occurrence; integer/ref range checks live in the lexer/parser, not the model.
* 2026-11-18 (wave 5) — Task 1.1 (phase 1) was pulled into the last phase-0 wave per the next-phase fill rule; it landed on `phase-0-infra` and ships in PR #1.
* 2026-11-18 (1.4/1.5) — A reference `n g R` with out-of-range numbers (n < 1 or g > 65535) is an error in the strict path, never a fallback to integers (lenient readers differ; repair path may relax). A lexer error inside the 2-token reference lookahead is an error.
* 2026-11-18 (2.2/2.6) — Local resource constants pending move into `Limits` (task 2.9): `maxRevisions := 4096` (revision-chain cap, reported as `limitExceeded .objects` for now) and repair `failBudget := 256` (failed parse attempts before the repair scan stops parsing). Accepted provisionally: they keep S3 bounds explicit and proven; the budget bounds attempts, not bytes per attempt.
* 2026-11-18 (1.13) — New CI dependency: apt `qpdf` on `ubuntu-24.04` (11.x, version asserted ≥ 11, not hash-pinned) for the differential job only; plus `actions/upload-artifact` v4.6.2 pinned by SHA. The trusted build job is unchanged.
* 2026-11-18 (wave 11) — Pulled 3.1 (Flate) and 3.2 (PNG predictors) from phase 3 ahead of the next-phase rule: they block 2.4 (xref streams), and Plan.md §6 Phase 2 says xref streams "need Flate + PNG predictors from Phase 3 — co-develop". They land on the current phase branch.
* 2026-11-18 (2.4a) — Hybrid files (§7.5.8.4): the classic table's entries take precedence; its `/XRefStm` stream fills the gaps (same as pdf.js/MuPDF). The XRefStm's own `/Prev`/`/XRefStm` are ignored. Unknown xref-stream entry types are treated as free (§7.5.8.3: references to them are null).
* 2026-11-19 (2.11) — `openFile` falls back to repair when strict fails, so Bogk `/Prev` cycles currently open as `repaired`. That contradicts M2 ("cyclic files all rejected") and Plan §10 item 3 ("fail fast and cleanly"); not weakened — M2 stays unticked until 2.13 makes cycle errors final. Corpus result on 91d9031: 74/74 open strict, 68,573/68,574 objects resolve (the 1 failure is a real duplicate key, rejected by the strict object parser per the 1.1 decision).
* 2026-11-19 (M2) — "Bogk-style cyclic files all rejected" is read as: xref-level cycles (`/Prev`, `/XRefStm`, object-stream self-containment) make `openFile` fail with `Error.cycle`; a cyclic `/Length` makes the affected object fail with `Error.cycle` while a file whose root is sound still opens (the cycle is in a stream, not in the file structure). Phase 4 decides whether a page needing such an object fails the page.
* 2026-11-19 (wave 16) — Plan.md open question "How much crypto to trust (M4): AES from vetted C (sandboxed) vs Lean implementation": default chosen = **Lean implementation** in `SafePdf/Core/Crypt/` (no FFI, no new dependency, matches Plan §10 layout `Core/Crypt.lean`), tested against published vectors, AES not proved (Plan §6 Phase 4). Revisit at M4 if performance or trust requires it; the human may override.
* 2026-11-19 (wave 16) — Task 3.8 added: Plan §6 Phase 3 asks for Flate "correctness via round-trip with a Lean deflate on random data", which no task covered. Phase 4 tasks 4.1 and 4.3a pulled into wave 16 (only 3.5 was ready in phase 3; no file overlap).
* 2026-11-19 (human) — Dev tooling: the human asked to install the Lean MCP server `lean-lsp-mcp` (uv 0.13.9 via Homebrew; `uvx lean-lsp-mcp`, unpinned, 0.33.0 at install) to speed up proof work; registered in `.mcp.json`, usage rules in CLAUDE.md. Not a project dependency (nothing in the lakefile). `lean-beam` considered and deferred (overlaps lean-lsp-mcp; beta; its checkpoint writes conflict with the build-isolation rule; own pinned toolchain).
* 2026-11-19 (M3) — Not ticked yet: every bomb is rejected within limits, but LZW bombs take 150–300 s under the default 256 MiB limit, which is not "fail fast" (Plan testing §3). M3 waits for 3.12 (and 3.11 helps Flate text-like data, ~6 MiB/s). Update 2026-11-19: 3.12 landed — every bomb now rejected in ≤ 3.3 s at default limits; M3 is ticked when phase 3's remaining tasks land.
* 2026-11-19 (3.11) — Flate work-bound constants changed with the table-driven decoder: `flateDecodeW_steps_le` is now steps ≤ 3360·input.size + 3·maxDecodedStreamBytes + 12163 (was 536·input.size + 3·max + 1923), because each dynamic block now builds two 512-entry lookup tables (cost model `tableCost = 5120`). Same theorem shape; larger constant, reported here rather than hidden. The cost model still counts one step per bit for table hits (an over-count).
* 2026-11-19 (4.3b) — Deviation: encryption-setup errors are final (rule E2, `openFile_setup_final`) so a password-protected file is never opened undecrypted via repair; they are recognised by message prefix (`Crypt.isSetupError`) because the task did not own `Error.lean`. This breaks the 2.13 rule "never classify errors by message text" — temporary; task 4.6 replaces it with a structured constructor. Resolved 2026-11-19 by 4.6 (`Error.encryption`).
* 2026-11-19 (human) — M4 clause "encrypted publisher PDFs open": the human confirmed the public-domain US government encrypted PDFs (NTSB, USCIS; corpus subset from 4.7) count as the publisher set.
* 2026-11-19 (4.5) — Budget theorems from 3.13 restated with larger preconditions now that `/Length` object-stream decodes are charged (their number is bounded only by the xref section size): `fetchCompressedB_cost` (fuel+1)·allowance, `fetchCompressedB_eq` (fuel+2)·allowance, `getObjectB_cost`/`validateB_cost` (objectCount+1)·allowance, `getObjectB_eq`/`validateB_eq` (objectCount+2)·allowance, `Session.getObject_eq` now a-posteriori, `strictOpenB_eq` lost its cost conjunct. Stated openly in each docstring; the a-posteriori `_eq_post` forms are the practical ones.
* 2026-11-19 (5.2b) — New resource cap: per-page form-expansion limit `Run.defaultMaxForms` = 100,000 (a cycle-free fan-out file otherwise expands 3.5 M forms in 31 s). Lives in `Core/Content/Run.lean` for now; to move into `Limits` with a `LimitKind` (task 6.4b covers moving font caps; add this one there).
* 2026-11-19 (6.6) — New CI dependency: apt `poppler-utils` (`pdftotext`, version asserted, not hash-pinned) in the `differential-objects` job, for the M6 text comparison. Plan.md names pdfium/MuPDF as the M6 reference; pdftotext (poppler) is used because it is in the ubuntu-24.04 archive and stdlib-only scripts can drive it — the M6 number is reported against pdftotext; a MuPDF (`mutool`) comparison can be added the same way if the human wants that exact reference. **Update 2026-11-19 (human): install pdftotext and also use MuPDF** — both are installed locally (Homebrew `poppler` 26.11.0, `mupdf-tools` 1.28.6) and in CI (apt `poppler-utils`, `mupdf-tools`, versions asserted, not hash-pinned); comparator `text-mupdf` (`mutool draw -q -F txt`) runs alongside `text`.

* 2026-11-20 (6.4b) — Font/form caps moved into `Limits` (`maxFontGlyphs`, `maxFontSubrs`, `glyphFuel`, `maxFormsPerPage`, defaults = the old constants). Only glyph-count and subr-count overflows return `limitExceeded` (new `LimitKind.fontGlyphs`/`fontSubrs`; previously `malformed`); running out of glyph fuel rejects the glyph and the form cap skips forms with a warning, like `maxRepairFailures` — failing the page instead would change M5 behaviour on `xobject-fanout.pdf`. The human may ask for the stricter reading.
* 2026-11-20 (7.1) — ℚ model uses core `Rat` (Init, v4.36); no new dependency. The Rotation type is `Doc.Rotation` (shared with the page tree). C2 proved over ℚ; the Fixed/float side has a one-step error bound only (Plan says "if feasible").
* 2026-11-20 (wave 26) — Task 7.1 (phase 7) pulled into a phase-6 wave per the next-phase fill rule (no file overlap); it lands on `phase-6-fonts-text` and ships in PR #7. A previous orchestrator session's interrupted wave 26 was discarded at the human's request ("start over"); its partial diffs were kept outside the repo only.

* 2026-11-20 (6.2b) — CFF glyph cap is a separate `Limits.maxCFFGlyphs` (65535, CID-keyed CJK fonts need it); Type 1 keeps `maxFontGlyphs` 4096 because it has no font-wide budget (65535 × glyphFuel ≈ 1.3·10⁹ steps), while CFF is bounded by `fontFuel` 2²⁵. CFF Subrs now share `maxFontSubrs` 32768 (was 65535 locally). Running out of `fontFuel` rejects the remaining glyphs (like `glyphFuel`), not an error.
* 2026-11-20 (7.2a) — Rasterizer coverage: integer device coordinates in 1/256 px with exact horizontal coverage and 4 vertical samples per pixel (configurable 1–16); flattening tolerance ≈ 0.1 px. The ℚ model is the proof reference; the hot path uses Int (Plan §8 "rational model as proof reference; float implementation tested" — here the implementation is exact integer arithmetic, proved equal to the model at subdivision points, rather than float).

* 2026-11-20 (6.7b) — Text fallback follows MuPDF (the M6 reference): no ASCII-at-code guess for non-Type 3 fonts (unknown glyph names → U+FFFD), spacing accents not composed with their base letter. Similarity vs pdftotext (poppler guesses ASCII and composes) may drop slightly; the human may prefer composing for readability. Multi-character glyph names (`SS`, negated relations) map to their full text where MuPDF prints U+FFFD.
* 2026-11-20 (7.2c) — Clip intersection uses the coverage product (MuPDF) rather than pointwise min; identical on fully covered/uncovered pixels, differs only on AA edges.

* 2026-11-20 (6.10) — Proposed reading of M6 clause 2 ("all glyph positions within tolerance"): 100% of glyph pairs matched with MuPDF (`positions` comparator) lie within max(1 pt, 0.1 × font size) of MuPDF's origin over the corpus, each exception triaged in `docs/deviations-from-spec.md`; unmatched characters are the text comparators' business. The human may set a different tolerance.
* 2026-11-20 (7.2d) — None of the raster caps is an error: all six degrade (documented per field); `maxPageSide` crops instead of failing so a page still shows at extreme zoom.
* 2026-11-20 (7.3a) — Full-page rendering entry point is `Raster.Glyph.renderPage`/`renderDocPage` (paths + text); `Clip.renderPaths` stays the path-only renderer (no hook added). Non-embedded standard-14 fonts draw nothing until substitute outlines exist (MuPDF uses URW fonts; adding font files needs a decision).

* 2026-11-20 (wave 31) — Phase 6 is waiting only on 6.8 (corpus access) and the M6 tick; to keep the loop productive, phase-7 tasks continue on `phase-6-fonts-text` (PR #7), as allowed by the next-phase fill rule. PR #7 therefore carries most of phase 7 so far; when M6 is ticked, phase 7's own branch is cut from this tip and its PR will contain only what lands after. The human may prefer to split differently.
* 2026-11-20 (7.3e) — Image edge anti-aliasing for rotated/skewed images (MuPDF draws hard edges) and per-axis box downscaling (MuPDF: tent filter, both axes) are deliberate small deviations from MuPDF; revisit if 7.4's pixel differential shows them costing matches.

* 2026-11-20 (7.4) — Proposed reading of M7 clause 1 ("within perceptual threshold"): a page is within when ≤ 1% of its pixels differ from MuPDF at 72 dpi under the 3×3-neighbourhood ± 32/255 rule (`pixels` comparator); M7 needs ≥ 95% of corpus pages within. Clause 2 uses the inspector's per-page compiled render time (excluding process start-up and file open). The human may set different thresholds.

* 2026-11-20 (7.2i) — The stroker's output changed slightly: chord pen offsets and tangents are rounded once to a 2⁻¹⁶ subpixel grid before polygon construction (error ≤ 2⁻¹⁷ subpixel unit per coordinate, `gridQ_err`), so the fast integer stroker and its `Rat` spec agree exactly; page rasters were unchanged on every measured scene (checksums equal).

* 2026-11-20 (8.1) — **GUI toolkit (Plan §8 open question, M8): Tcl/Tk 9 through a small C shim** (default chosen by the loop; the human may override). Rationale: the sibling project bib-lean already has a working Lean ↔ Tk shell (`c/tk_shim.c`, proved Tcl quoting so no data is ever spliced into script text); Tk 9.0 + Xvfb are installed in this sandbox (lean-tools kit) and Homebrew `tcl-tk` on macOS; a photo image in a canvas is enough to show the core's RGBA pages; widgets for menus, scrollbars, outline tree, search entry exist; the shell stays unverified and replaceable (Plan §8). Alternatives: GTK 4 (heavier C API, more FFI surface), Qt (C++), SDL2 (no widgets), Tauri/webview (rejected by the threat model: no browser engine near document data). Consequence: `lakefile.toml` becomes `lakefile.lean` (`extern_lib` exists only in the Lean DSL) — task 8.2a.

* 2026-11-20 (7.6a) — JPEG decoding is a pure-Lean decoder in `SafePdf/Quarantine/` (Plan §8 decided "FFI only in the quarantine zone for DCT/JPX"; a Lean decoder needs no FFI and no new dependency, and it is held to the Core hygiene rules). Reference behaviour is libjpeg-turbo 2.1 as linked by the installed MuPDF (bit-exact on fixtures). JPX/JBIG2/CCITT remain placeholders.

* 2026-11-20 (Phase 9) — **SUPERSEDED the same day by the human (Plan.md S8/C7, Phase 9 "Persistence — annotations inside the PDF"): annotations are written into the PDF by incremental update to a new revision (`FILE-annotated.pdf` or bib-lean's PUT), original bytes a prefix, never in place; XFDF is import/export only.** Original entry: **User annotations persist in an XFDF sidecar** (ISO 19444-1), never in the PDF. The usual in-file format — annotation objects appended by incremental update (ISO 32000 §12.5) — needs a PDF writer and would end "read-only by construction" (S8); XFDF is the standard interchange XML that Acrobat and others import/export, so the notes stay portable. An "export annotated copy" writer may be a later, separate decision; in-place writes never.

* 2026-11-20 (7.7) — **Substitute fonts are found on the system, not committed.** The human asked to fix a page whose non-embedded Helvetica rendered blank, which settles the Blocked fallback-font question in favour of painting substitutes. The repository is MIT, so no font files are added to it: the Shell loads URW base-35 (AGPL-3.0 with font exception; same outlines MuPDF bundles → pixel parity) or TeX Gyre (GUST licence) from `SAFEPDF_FONT_DIR` or the standard Debian font directories at run time; without them, non-embedded text stays unpainted as before. CI needs `apt-get install fonts-urw-base35` (workflow patch; the push token lacks `workflow` scope).
* 2026-11-20 (7.8) — CCITT fax decoding (Plan "Tier 2 / later": CCITT fax + JBIG2 in Lean) is pulled forward into Phase 7 because Ghostscript's Type 3 bitmap fonts in the corpus are CCITT-encoded; it lives in `SafePdf/Quarantine/CCITT.lean` next to DCT under the Core hygiene rules, no FFI. JBIG2 stays Tier 2.
* 2026-11-20 (Phase 10) — The human made this repository a Lake dependency of bib-lean (pinned by commit, built from source); `viewer` must stay a non-default target and the Lean pin must stay v4.36.0 in step with bib-lean. This repository has no Batteries dependency; Plan's "Batteries v4.36.0 tag in step" is read as: if one is ever added it must be that tag (adding one still needs its own Decisions entry, ground rule 2).
* 2026-11-20 (9.5a) — Incremental-update writer rules: new objects numbered from the old `/Size`; each affected page is rewritten under its own number with a direct `/Annots` (an indirect `/Annots` array is never rewritten — other pages may share it); the update's xref kind follows the newest original section (classic → classic; xref stream → an uncompressed xref stream listing itself; hybrid → classic without `/XRefStm`, old hybrid reached via `/Prev`); `/Root` `/Info` carried over, second `/ID` = MD5(old ID ‖ new object bytes); the writer re-parses its own output strictly before returning bytes; encrypted and repaired inputs are refused for now (`Error.unsupported`; encryption support → 9.5f).
* 2026-11-20 (9.5b) — Writer rule W9: `writeAnnots limits doc marks (aux := [])` also writes auxiliary stream objects (appearance streams), numbered after the new annotations, generation 0, `/Length` set by the writer and checked on re-parse; all C7 theorems hold for any `aux`. A highlight with a comment follows pdf.js (`/Contents` + `/Popup` with `/Parent`, no `/IRT`); a note anchored to a selection keeps its quads in a non-standard `/QuadPoints` on the `/Text` annotation (ignored by other readers, used by us to restore the anchor).
* 2026-11-20 (9.5f) — Writer rule W10 (supersedes 9.5a's "encrypted inputs are refused"): encrypted documents are annotated. New objects are encrypted with the document's handler and file key (empty user password): Algorithm 1 object keys; RC4/AESV2/AESV3 by `/StrF`/`/StmF`; `/Identity` untouched; exceptions mirror decryption; xref streams never encrypted; `/Encrypt` and first `/ID` carried (also in xref-stream trailers). AES IV is deterministic because the pure core has no randomness: SHA-256("safe-pdf 9.5f IV" ‖ file key ‖ num ‖ gen ‖ string counter ‖ kind ‖ plaintext)[0..16). Acceptable: CBC needs distinct IVs for distinct plaintexts under one key, which holds; readers take the IV from the data (§7.6.3); with an empty user password confidentiality against holders of the file is nil anyway; the only leak is identical ciphertext for an unchanged string at the same position of a re-written object. That encryption inverts decryption is tested (and checked by the writer's self-check on every write), not proved — it rests on the vector-tested RC4/AES primitives. Repaired inputs stay refused.
* 2026-11-20 (7.9b) — A form XObject cached by the per-page form cache is charged to the decoded-bytes budget once per page run instead of once per `Do`. The budget theorems (`Filter.decodeAllB_total`, `Doc.decodeContentsB_total`) bound the bytes actually decoded and stay true unchanged; the visible effect is that a page whose repeated decodes used to exhaust the budget now draws those forms (documented in the `Run.lean` module doc; test `run-cache` case 5).
* 2026-11-20 (9.5g) — Writer rule W11: `writeUpdate limits doc edits sups aux` generalizes `writeAnnots` (the add-only case; all 9.5a/b/f statements unchanged). Per page: refs to remove (each on the old list) and annotations to add; `/Annots` = old-without-removed (order kept, superseded refs in place) ++ new refs. Supersessions re-emit an existing object under its own number and generation (ISO 32000-2 §7.5.6): below the old `/Size`, not a page object, a dictionary under that generation in the original, at most once; a page with only superseded annotations is not rewritten; superseded objects take no new numbers, are encrypted under their own number (W10) and fetched back by the self-check (W8). Proved: `c7Update`. Annotation layer A10/A11: a kept popup is superseded, an added comment gets a new popup object, a dropped comment's popup ref is removed; popup `/NM` = `safepdf-mark-ID-popup-CID`. The shell writes comments as `/Contents` + `/Popup`, still reads 9.5c's separate `/Text` comments, and renders edited saved marks from an in-memory preview update.
* 2026-11-20 (7.6d) — JPEG polish (reduced-size decoding when an image is drawn at ≤ ½ — a speed optimisation — and libjpeg-turbo SIMD IDCT parity on overflowing coefficients of corrupt files) moves out of Phase 7 into Plan.md's "Tier 2 / later". Reason: neither affects M7 (99%+ of corpus pages within the threshold; no corpus page needs it); the render-time tail is handled by 7.9–7.9c. Phase 7's exit criterion is checked without it.
* 2026-11-20 (9.11) — XFDF export writes a highlight's comment as `<contents>` inside the `<highlight>` plus `<popup name="safepdf-mark-H-popup-C" open="no"/>` (matching 9.5g's `/Contents` + `/Popup` in the PDF); the comment takes its highlight's colour; import still reads 9.5d's separate-`<text>` form; imported ids are kept when ≥ the pre-import `nextId` and not yet taken, and added marks are appended in id order.
* 2026-11-20 (8.5) — Reading of M8 "daily-drivable viewer on the corpus" (proposed by the loop; the human may change it): every corpus file opens; every page is displayed; no crash or timeout; no event or page over 5 s; p95 time-to-display ≤ 500 ms; peak RSS ≤ 1 GiB per file; the scripted session (outline, search, select + copy, highlight + note, save, reopen) passes on 3 papers incl. an encrypted one. Measured by `scripts/viewer-corpus.sh` (`docs/viewer-corpus.md`). The thresholds were fixed before looking at the data and are not moved to fit it.
* 2026-11-20 (9.5e2) — Writer self-check (W8/W10) also requires `sameState`: the re-opened result's decryption state equals the original's in every field (handler incl. `/EncryptMetadata` and crypt filters, file key, `encRef`, `permsOk`). It only rejects bad output, never changes bytes. Chosen over proving the xref/trailer parse-back (not yet proved; would also leave superseded indirect `/Encrypt` objects open).
* 2026-11-20 (7.10) — Reduced-size JPEG decoding uses one reduction step less than MuPDF (`dctScale l = min 3 (l − 1)`): MuPDF finishes the shrink with a tent filter, our image placement with a box filter that barely smooths with fewer than two samples per device pixel; MuPDF's exact factor made 15 of 21 changed pages slightly worse and pushed arxiv-1105.2345 p8 to 1.08%. Not reduced: images also used in tiling patterns or Type 3 glyphs, images whose soft mask has `/Matte`, soft masks, inline images.
* 2026-11-20 (9.9) — Annotation rules A12–A15: FreeText as pdf.js writes it (`/Contents`, `/DA (/Helv N Tf r g b rg)`, `/F 4`, `/Border [0 0 0]`, no `/C`; box from Helvetica widths, line height 1.35×size, 2 pt padding; appearance with a standard-14 `/Helv` WinAnsi font clipped to the box); Ink with `/InkList` (one array per stroke), `/BS /W`, `/C`, `/CA` only below 1, round caps/joins; edits supersede (W11). Characters outside WinAnsi: exact text kept in `/Contents` (UTF-16, what readers extract), the appearance draws `·` for each missing glyph (MuPDF's replacement), nothing embedded. The text/draw colour pop-over offers six swatches (black, red, blue, green, orange, purple) instead of PDF.js's native colour input (deterministic and testable).

* 2026-11-23 (Phase 10) — **bib-lean reader mode descoped at the human's request**: the integration will be implemented on the bib-lean side (safe-pdf consumed as a library / external reader there); Phase 10 here is viewer polish only. Tasks 10.1–10.4 are archived; their code (`SafePdf/Shell/App/BibLean/`, `--biblean`, `docs/biblean.md`, `Tests/biblean/`) stays as-built until bib-lean's replacement lands — removing it is a separate decision.

* 2026-11-23 (10.6) — Click-away: a press on the page canvas keeps the Highlight and Add text/Draw pop-overs open (Plan.md says every transient closes on a click elsewhere). Reason: PDF.js keeps editor parameter toolbars open while the mode is on, and closing them on page clicks breaks restyling a just-selected mark. Presses anywhere else and Escape close them. Reversible: drop `pagePath` from `.hlPop`/`.edPop` in `Transient.keepPaths`.
* 2026-11-23 (10.7) — Undo history is cleared on every open, including the reopen after Save (saved marks read back rounded and with new ids, so older steps would not line up with the file); cap 100 steps.
* 2026-11-23 (10.9) — macOS key choices follow Preview / the HIG where PDF.js has none: ⌥⌘G go to page, ⌃⌘S sidebar, ⌃⌘H highlight (⌘H is Hide), ⌘[/⌘] history, ⌘. cancel; X11 keys keep working on macOS; ⌘, and ⌘? unbound (no preferences/help).

* 2026-11-23 (M10) — M10 ticked on macOS evidence; the human runs the Linux smoke later (their words 2026-11-23: "you can have different stuff for linux and for mac, that's fine, I'll run on linux later").

* 2026-11-23 (11.1) — The macOS shell talks to the core through a plain C API (`macos/safepdf_core.h`) implemented in C over Lean `@[export]`s (`SafePdf/Shell/Ffi.lean`); exports own their arguments (v4.36 forbids `@&`); documents are `lean_mark_mt`'d and shared across threads; `libsafepdfffi.a` comes from the non-default target `macosFfi` and is linked with Lean's runtime by the system clang; AppKit provides native scrolling, momentum and magnification. Spike numbers: init 42–57 ms, open ~5 ms, 70–200 ms per page at 2×. Parity (11.3) waits for 11.2 and the human's feel check.
* 2026-11-23 (12.4) — Writer AES IVs come from an IV seed passed into the pure writer (empty = the 9.5f IV); the viewer passes 64 fresh bytes per save and refuses to save an encrypted file without randomness; all `Spec.Write` theorems hold for every seed. RC4 inputs are written with the document's handler plus a note; signed inputs with a note that the signature covers only the original revision; `/DocMDP` P1/P2 certified files are refused (export XFDF), P3 written with the note. Review package: `docs/crypto-review.md`.

* 2026-11-23 (12.1) — One capped read (`SafePdf.Shell.Read`) for every whole-file input read; `SafePdf.Api` imports this Shell module (its only IO); each site keeps its existing cap. Strict mode never runs repair (proved via `RepairLog.attempts = 0`); `Api.Error.repairRefused` now carries the strict error — an API change.
* 2026-11-23 (12.5) — Copy includes invisible text (OCR layers are the real text of scanned pages) with a status count of the characters not visible on the page; the inspector flags hidden runs in JSON only on pages that have them.
* 2026-11-23 (12.6) — lean4lean is pinned by commit (23fd034, toolchain v4.36.0) as a CI tool, not a Lake dependency; the CI changes live in one patch, `docs/ci-pending.patch`, until a token with `workflow` scope applies it.

* 2026-11-23 (12.2a) — The document worker is a separate default-target executable (`worker`) speaking a length-prefixed binary protocol over stdin/stdout; it receives document bytes, never paths, and runs with an empty environment and cwd `/`; until 12.2b it still reads the system font directories for substitute fonts.
* 2026-11-23 (12.7d) — `/Length` and object-stream references to free and to missing objects now fail with one message (user-visible in error texts), so C4 holds with exact equality on files with free entries.

* 2026-11-23 (12.2b) — The document worker confines itself before reading any request: empty environment required (exit 3), substitute fonts read at startup, then macOS `sandbox_init` `(deny default)` with nothing allowed or Linux no_new_privs + seccomp allowlist (untested on Linux); any failure exits 4; no unconfined mode in `worker` (the test-only `worker-probe` has one).
* 2026-11-23 (12.2c) — The Tk viewer renders pages, thumbnails and the outline through one worker per document (20 s deadline, kill and respawn); it falls back to in-process rendering with a status note when the `worker` executable is missing (dev builds); selection text, links and layered renders stay in-process until 12.2d. Test settings reach the worker as flags only (`--test-stall-ms`).

* 2026-11-23 (12.7e) — Repair rule R6 reads scanned object streams on unencrypted files; the C4 theorems gain hypothesis H7 (no compressed entry after repair) — conclusions unchanged, H7 shown necessary.
* 2026-11-23 (12.2d) — The worker's startup configuration (limits, substitute-font search) arrives as command-line flags parsed before confinement; the viewer's selection/search index, links and layered renders come from the worker.

* 2026-11-24 (12.3) — The per-operation budget is a viewer-side reservation pool (512 MiB, invariant in the structure) for work in flight; a search shares one page/time allowance; worker fences sit outside Lean (Linux rlimits; macOS a `phys_footprint` watchdog because RLIMIT_AS/DATA are rejected; RLIMIT_CPU 60 s per request beside the 20 s deadline); Tk photos, Save/export and the worker's per-page decode budget are outside the pool (12.3b).

* 2026-11-24 (10.c1) — CI viewer job installs apt `fonts-urw-base35` and `x11-xserver-utils` (CI packages, not Lake dependencies); the viewer supports Tk 8.6 (Ubuntu) through a PNG tint fallback.
* 2026-11-24 (12.3b) — Page photos get their own pool; the per-page image cap drops from 2²⁶ px to 10 Mpx so deep zoom stays within the 1024 MiB viewer ceiling (600/800 % show at 344 % on a 1.334-scaling Mac, with a status note) — pending the human's choice between this cap and tiled rendering (12.3c).
* 2026-11-24 (12.3c) — The human keeps the 10 Mpx per-page cap (600/800 % display at ~344 % on a 1.334-scaling Mac); no tiled rendering.
* 2026-11-24 (11.2) — **Native macOS shell architecture and design brief.** The human: the port keeps the same design as the Linux version but should feel new (updated modern design, seamless use). Decided: the shared Lean logic crosses the C API as pure functions and flat C structs — anything with a theorem or `#guard` (View zoom/reanchor, Select bands/hit tests, Marks + undo, Save/C7, the Menu/Toolbar tables) stays in Lean, so the proofs keep applying; the shell is Swift (swiftc from the command-line tools, no Xcode project) and owns only widgets and events; rendering, text index, links and outline go through the confined document worker (12.2a–d) with priorities, cancellation and deadline kill — the native shell is sandboxed and never stalls from day one, and the spike's in-process render path (single thread, no cancellation, document leak) is retired, not fixed. Modern treatment: unified `NSToolbar` + SF Symbols with macOS 26 Liquid Glass runtime-gated (min macOS 14), sidebar material, native momentum + live pinch with a cross-fade to the sharp re-render, dark mode, accent-coloured selection, sheets/popovers, `SafePdf.app` bundle. Full plan: `docs/macos-design.md`; tasks 11.3a–11.6.
* 2026-11-24 (11.3d1) — The macOS app keeps the Tk rule that external (URI) links are never opened: the confirm sheet shows the full address with **Copy Address / Cancel**; no `NSWorkspace.open`. The wave brief had asked for an Open button; the agent followed the existing rule (`Viewer.confirmUri`, `docs/viewer.md`). Changing it would be a security-policy change for the human.
* 2026-11-24 (11.5) — The native smoke is an in-process scripted session (`SmokeSession.swift`) instead of an Accessibility-driven one: AX needs the human to grant Accessibility to the driving process and a visible window, which the background discipline forbids. Feel items (momentum, pinch, real-screen moves, Liquid Glass, VoiceOver) move to the human's 11.6 pass.
* 2026-11-24 (human, 11.6) — Decisions after the manual check: minimum **macOS 15** (Lean 4.36's runtime libraries are built for 15); the `macos` CI job does **not** run the Lean test suite (exercised by the Linux jobs); the attachment-probe gap (no 16 MB cap, tooltip after a probe) is **accepted**; external links stay **Copy Address only** (never opened); ⌘X/⌘V cut and paste marks. VoiceOver for page content not requested.

* 2026-11-25 (human) — **Phase 13 — Hardening II is the next phase** (Plan.md §6 Phase 13, M13; tasks 13.1–13.21). The human asked for a plan from the check of the S1 and S3 arguments against the compiled binary (2026-11-24/25). That check ran on a checkout at 031d779, before Phases 11–12 were pulled; the plan was rebuilt on `main` at 430203d: what Phase 12 already delivered is not repeated (capped reads, the confined worker and its deadline, budgets, IVs, honest UI, C4, raster error bounds, lean4lean, compiled-vs-interpreted), and one item was added from reading the merged code — both GUI processes still parse and write documents themselves (13.12–13.16; 12.2c/12.2d had recorded it as remaining). Plan.md §4 and §7 will be reworded by 13.21; the human agreed with this plan. Not in the phase: the external review (12.9, deferred), sandboxing the GUI process, crypto functional correctness, a hostile-sample corpus, OSS-Fuzz onboarding (docs/safety.md §5 items 7, 3, 11, 10).

* 2026-11-26 (human) — **Phase 14 — Signature verification, full scope** (Plan.md §6 Phase 14, M14; tasks 14.1–14.14): RSA and ECDSA, modern (`adbe.pkcs7.detached`, `ETSI.CAdES.detached`) and legacy (`adbe.pkcs7.sha1`, `adbe.x509.rsa_sha1`) SubFilters all in-phase. Verification only — signing needs key custody, a different threat model. Design rules: the core verdict is pure (trust anchors and the current time enter as data, S4 holds); it runs in the confined worker; revocation and timestamp fetching need network and stay out (Tier 2), reported honestly as "revocation not checked"; the rationale is that the historical signature CVEs are structure attacks (wrapping, shadow attacks), which the revision model and `Spec/Unambiguous` can close with the coverage theorem (14.7). Trust-anchor sourcing (and whether to bundle AATL/EUTL) is decided at 14.11, asked in Blocked. Queues behind Phase 13.

* 2026-11-27 (human) — **Phase 15 — Printing** (Plan.md §6 Phase 15; tasks 15.1–15.7; issue [#21](https://github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/safe-pdf/issues/21)): print the rendered pixels, never the input file — handing the document to CUPS would run another unverified parser (Ghostscript/poppler) over hostile input outside our sandbox; the spool is an image-only PDF generated from the verified rasterizer's output (Dangerzone's reconstruction idea applied to printing), with an honest status line naming the dpi. 300 dpi default (fits the 10 Mpx page cap); 600 dpi needs banded rendering and is deferred. Linux: our own dialog + `lp`; macOS: `NSPrintOperation` (native panel). Print to PDF included. Queues behind Phase 14. GitHub issues opened the same day tracking [#19](https://github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/safe-pdf/issues/19)'s feature list: printing [#21](https://github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/safe-pdf/issues/21), signature verification [#22](https://github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/safe-pdf/issues/22) (Phase 14), password-protected PDFs [#23](https://github.com/Beneficial-AI-Foundation/safe-pdf/issues/23) (unscheduled; its key derivation, `/P` semantics, Api/worker password plumbing and prompt dialogs are scoped in the issue).

* 2026-11-28 (human) — **Printing moved to later work.** Phase 15 is no longer a phase: its design sits in Plan.md §6 under "Tier 2 / later" ("Printing (deferred)"), its tasks 15.1–15.7 under "Deferred by the human", and its branch row is removed, so the loop ends after Phase 14. Consistent with the Print button's removal from both GUIs on 2026-11-27 (PR #24, on `main`); `Toolbar.Tool.print` stays for its return.

* 2026-11-28 (13.2) — `Std.TreeSet` (`import Std.Data.TreeSet`) is now used in `Core/` as the ordered key set `ObjParse.KeySet` for the duplicate-key check. It ships with the pinned Lean 4.36.0 toolchain, so there is no new Lake dependency; key order is `compare` on `List UInt8`. Not a hash set (unseeded hashes can be collided). `hasKey` stays as the specification; `DictAgreeN` gained the `sn`/`SeenOf` parameters, `parseObject_print` is unchanged; `Spec/PrefixStable.lean` was touched only for the extra `parseDict` argument.
* 2026-11-28 (13.9) — `Shell/Worker/Protocol.lean` joins the strict set (`STRICT_TARGETS` in `scripts/hygiene.py`): it passes the source and environment scans unchanged (no partial, extern, unsafe or IO), and fixture `src-protocol-partial` shows a `partial` there fails the guard. The environment scan also covers definitions and `IO`-in-type (S4) with one allowed exception, `SafePdf.Api.openFile`.
* 2026-11-28 — 13.4 split into 13.4a (calibration spike, no library change), 13.4b (core allowance), 13.4c (stacked-clip masks and `ClipCache.find?`), 13.4d (worker and viewers); 13.5 now needs 13.4b–13.4d. The unit, default and caller-visible behaviour are decided after 13.4a's numbers and recorded here.
* 2026-11-28 (13.4a) — **Render work allowance decided** from `docs/render-work-calibration.md` (81 files, 2,450 pages, tool `scripts/corpus/render-work.py`; the tool's renderer paints the same image as `renderDocPage` on 2,450 of 2,450 pages): **unit = steps** (the quantity `renderItemsCounted_steps_le` and `fillEdgesCounted` already bound; it includes the edge-walking term pixels cannot see — a 5,000-sliver fill is 32.6M steps against 0.95M pixels); **default = 128 steps per page pixel with a floor of 2^20**, a `Limits` field (Letter at 72 dpi: 62,042,112 = 4.06× the heaviest corpus page, 15.3M steps; at 144 dpi: 248,168,448 = 6.91× the 144 dpi maximum 35.9M; 0 corpus pages hit it at either dpi; a per-pixel factor because page work grows 1.03–3.98× for a 4× larger area); **caller sees**: the core returns the image and the stop reason together (`limitExceeded .renderWork` plus the pixels so far, marked incomplete); `Api.renderPage` keeps its signature and returns the error, so `renderPage_size` still means a complete page; a new `Api.renderPagePartial` returns the partial image; the worker answers with a completeness flag (13.4d) and the viewers show the raster with a "drawn partly" message and do not retry. **Deviation from the 13.4 row:** the allowance is checked inside the clip-mask recursion and per fill, not only between items, because `clip-stack-2000.pdf` is a single item costing 1.448G steps (5.76G at 144 dpi); so the theorem is "allowance plus one inner unit" and not "plus one item's". The calibration is for papers and reports (74 arXiv, 6 NTSB, 1 USCIS), not maps or CAD; the field is a `Limits` knob.

* 2026-11-20 (7.3a/6.12) — **Substitute fonts for non-embedded fonts?** Our renderer draws nothing for non-embedded fonts (standard 14, the Times-Roman "hail mary" fallback, non-embedded TrueType/CID), while MuPDF paints them with bundled URW base-35 / Noto fonts. Many corpus pages (e.g. government PDFs, older arXiv) use non-embedded Helvetica/Times, so M7's pixel match will suffer. Options: (a) bundle the URW base-35 Type 1 fonts (AGPL-3.0 with a font exception, from ghostscript/urw-base35-fonts) as data parsed by our Type 1 parser — adds font files to the repo/binary and a licence to review; (b) bundle a permissively licensed set (e.g. TeX Gyre (GUST font licence) or Liberation (OFL)) with metric-compatible shapes; (c) leave them unpainted and count those pages against M7. The loop will default to (c) and continue; please choose. **Answered 2026-11-20**: the human asked to fix a blank non-embedded-Helvetica page → task 7.7, see Decisions (fonts loaded from the system, not committed).
